This is, of course, absolutely necessary, an urgent requirement for the construction of appropriate practical structures in the extramural field – group practices, day clinics and expansion of health professions, the creation of a catalogue of services for established doctors – so that the services provided in the ambulance – diagnosis and therapy – can also be carried out in the periphery, and the number of contractual doctors must also be increased. Outsourcing from the stationary area could primarily be done by avoiding hospitalization through barriers in the form of ambulances, as well as by a better organization in the stationary area. It is very important to install a management that is also capable of bringing patients to diagnosis and therapy as quickly as possible. The real question in the health field, ladies and gentlemen, concerns something quite different. The real question is: Are we going towards a state health system, with uniform contributions, with uniform benefits, probably also with rationalizations like in England, with waiting times and the like, in other words, towards a system that then no longer works, or do we stick to a system as we have it, namely a self-government system? [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I'll tell you: This system is closer to citizens, it is more continuous, it is more cost-oriented – but we need reform. If all this and some other structural changes, whose necessity we see, are made, then we no longer need to discuss the question of whether we have a two-class system in the health field, because then we have a functioning, highly reasonable self-government.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-04-04-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00068_d7e10002	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! High house! This is not an honest policy. However, an honest policy does not shut the eyes of the fact that there are certain solidarity communities within which the financial compensation must take place. Mr Nürnberger – he is not here at the moment – was bitterly angry at me a while ago when I dared to recall from this point the ambiguity that every schilling, which a pensioner receives more, must be taken away at the same time by an active person within the community defined by the payment procedure. Analogs apply in respect of the solidarity of the healthy and the sick as part of our health insurance: No ambulance visit is free. If I do not myself contribute to the use of a benefit, others will have to pay for me – or myself, by detour of general higher health insurance contributions. This has also been clearly expressed in the previous debate, and this, Mr President, is also the background to finding an adequate solution to the taxation of accident pensions. High house! But the problem is deeper. It is that there is a feedback effect in this system. Man now tends to deal with something that seems to cost nothing more careless. A certain cost contribution – in any form, whether it be self-sustained or as a fee – therefore has a double health effect: He makes aware that I use an expensive performance, and he reduces the willingness to use this performance easily. So, as you say today, he has a steering effect. However, this contribution reduces the total cost of the system, which is not only in the interest of all members of the Solidarity Community, but also reduces the budget. Therefore, an honest opposition policy should support all steps taken by the Federal Government to relieve solidarity communities. Instead, in all the opposition speeches we have been experiencing for months a daily giant sand-in-the-eye scatter, raising a demand for free or increased funding for any social group after another, without ever adding that this will only burden the general public even more. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] High house! Let me conclude by saying a word on perhaps the most important solidarity, the solidarity between generations, as a senior representative. This solidarity must also be seen not statically, but dynamically. In the sense of this dynamic view, the Austrian Seniors' Federation feels responsible not only for the seniors of today, but also for the seniors of tomorrow – the youth who populates the gallery in large numbers. The Senior Pensions Federation is therefore in favour of a long-term sustainable pension scheme, which is also intended to ensure an honorable pension for today's active and today's youth. It is also necessary to clarify that the older generation is not only recipients and consumers of services, but also to a large extent provides services that contribute significantly to the cohesion of generations. A responsible policy must strive not to tear down graves, but to overcome them by building bridges within the solidarity communities with reason over which the partners can then find themselves.

There are many couples in Austria who are unwantedly childless. They speak of up to 30,000 couples who wish for children, but for various reasons they cannot or can only receive medical help. Unfortunately, this number continues to rise, and the causes are of a wide variety of nature. The experts puzzle about it and ask themselves: Is it environmental influences, is it stress, is it wrong nutrition, is it lack of exercise, or is it just the fact that many women are waiting longer and longer for family planning for professional reasons? In any case, this is a very negative appearance of our prosperity world. Ladies and gentlemen! Medical treatments of such couples cost a lot of money. For example, in vitro fertilization costs between 50 000 and 100 000 S per treatment. These costs had to be paid by the affected couples themselves by the end of 1999 because such treatment was not considered to be the treatment of a disease according to the applicable legal situation or the case law. With 1. In January 2000, the previous government of the so-called IVF funds for the financing of in vitro-fertilization treatments introduced gratifyingly. [[Abg. Dr. Hannes Bauer: But you didn't do that!]] ==References====External links== It has been extended again in March 2001 and is glad to see it extended again. In other words, the duration of the financing agreement has been extended: 35 percent pay the social security, 35 percent pay the health ministry and 30 percent pay the affected couples themselves. After one year of the existence of this IVF fund, a positive balance can be drawn. There were about 3,000 couples who received financial support from treatment attempts in 2000. Ladies and gentlemen! Given the dramatic development of birth rate – it has fallen to incredible 1.34 in 1998; Mr Donabauer has already mentioned this before me – this extension of the duration of the IVF Fund, in addition to the introduction of the childcare allowance, is certainly a very important family policy measure. The government shows that it knows what children are worth to us, that it knows that children are the foundation of our future, and that they do not allow two-class medicine in Austria in particular.

A large insurance group relocated its software development from the agglomeration, for example, to Raabs an der Thaya, because the overall cost structure is much cheaper there; say: cheaper rents and highly motivated staff. In the past, the exact opposite was the case in the administrative field. Such past developments must be stopped in the future. A positive example of how synergies can be used in the administrative field is the Waidhofen Service Centre on the Thaya, where under a roof there are Gendarmerie, Farm Chamber, District Court, Survey Office and similar facilities. The citizen has a contact point in his immediate vicinity, and facilities such as an event hall and the technical infrastructure can be shared. Thus, optimum use of space and technical resources is possible, and thus a favourable cost structure is given. Today, under Chapter 15, the most important budget items, social affairs, were discussed, an area which is very sensitive because of its direct impact on people.
